                                                                              (Geology after Beik, 2005)


                                                                                                Qf Artificial fill (Historical) − Engineered fill used in the construction of Interstate
                                                                                                15 and other road and railroad beds ;larger areas of fill used to create new level
                                                                                                building areas are also mapped based on aerial photographs taken in May 2002;
                                                                                                although only larger fill deposits are shown, fill of variable composition may be
                                                                                                present in any developed area; variable thickness up to about 90 feet (30 m) ...

                                                                                                Qfd Disturbed land (Historical) – Land disturbed by sand and gravel operations;
                                                                                                only the larger operations are mapped and their outlines are based on aerial
                                                                                                photographs taken in May 2002; land within these areas contains a complex,
                                                                                                rapidly changing mix of cuts and fills, as well as excellent exposures of Lake
                                                                                                Bonneville sediments. Disturbed land at the top of the east Traverse Mountains is
                                                                                                also mapped based on aerial photographs taken in May 2002; this area contains
                                                                                                a complex mix of very large cuts and areas of fill in deeply weathered volcanic
                                                                                                rocks of the east Traverse Mountains (Tv) ...

                                                                                                Qmsy, Qmso - Landslide deposits (Historical to Pleistocene) − Very poorly
                                                                                                sorted, clay- to boulder-size, locally derived material deposited by rotational and
                                                                                                translational movement; characterized by hummocky topography, numerous
                                                                                                internal scarps, and chaotic bedding attitudes; basal slip surfaces... younger
                                                                                                landslides (Qmsy) may have historical movement, but typically are characterized
                                                                                                by slightly to moderately subdued landslide features indicative of middle or late
                                                                                                Holocene to Late Pleistocene age; older landslides (Qmso) are deeply incised
                                                                                                and head scarps and hummocky topography have been extensively modified by
                                                                                                erosion and so most are likely Late Pleistocene in age...

                                                                                                Qac - Alluvial and colluvial deposits (Holocene to Upper Pleistocene) − Poorly
                                                                                                to moderately sorted, generally poorly stratified, clay- to boulder-size, locally
                                                                                                derived sediment deposited in swales, small drainages, and the upper reaches of
                                                                                                larger ephemeral streams by fluvial, slope-wash, and creep processes...

                                                                                                Tv - Volcanic rocks of the east Traverse Mountains, undivided (Oligocene-
                                                                                                Eocene) - Complexly interbedded block and ash-flow tuffs, volcanic mudflow
                                                                                                breccia, minor lava flows, and minor fluvial volcaniclastic deposits...block and
                                                                                                ash-flow tuffs likely comprise the bulk of these deposits...

       Error Codes

              The following errors were encountered during the computation:

              -101 = Only one (or zero) surface / slope intersections.
              -103 = Two surface / slope intersections, but one or more surface / nonslope external polygon intersections lie between them. This usually occurs when the slip surface extends past the bottom of the soil region, but may also occur on a benched slope model with two
              sets of Slope Limits.
              -104 = Same as -102. Surface / nonslope intersections also exist, but these points lie outside the arc defined by the two surface / slope intersections.
              -105 = More than two surface / slope intersections with no valid slip surface.
              -115 = Surface too shallow, below the minimum depth.
              -1000 = No valid slip surfaces are generated at a grid center. Unable to draw a surface.

       Error Codes

              The following errors were encountered during the computation:

              -101 = Only one (or zero) surface / slope intersections.
              -103 = Two surface / slope intersections, but one or more surface / nonslope external polygon intersections lie between them. This usually occurs when the slip surface extends past the bottom of the soil region, but may also occur on a benched slope model with two
              sets of Slope Limits.
              -104 = Same as -102. Surface / nonslope intersections also exist, but these points lie outside the arc defined by the two surface / slope intersections.
              -105 = More than two surface / slope intersections with no valid slip surface.
              -115 = Surface too shallow, below the minimum depth.
              -1000 = No valid slip surfaces are generated at a grid center. Unable to draw a surface.

          Error Codes

                 The following errors were encountered during the computation:

                 -99 = Slip surface intersects an infinite strength material. If infinite strength regions are defined for a model, a large number of potential slip surfaces may show this error code. This is Normal.
                 -103 = Two surface / slope intersections, but one or more surface / nonslope external polygon intersections lie between them. This usually occurs when the slip surface extends past the bottom of the soil region, but may
                 also occur on a benched slope model with two sets of Slope Limits.
                 -106 = Average slice width is less than 0.0001 * (maximum horizontal extent of soil region). This limitation is imposed to avoid numerical errors which may result from too many slices, or too small a slip region.

          Error Codes

                 The following errors were encountered during the computation:

                 -99 = Slip surface intersects an infinite strength material. If infinite strength regions are defined for a model, a large number of potential slip surfaces may show this error code. This is Normal.
                 -103 = Two surface / slope intersections, but one or more surface / nonslope external polygon intersections lie between them. This usually occurs when the slip surface extends past the bottom of the soil region, but may
                 also occur on a benched slope model with two sets of Slope Limits.
                 -106 = Average slice width is less than 0.0001 * (maximum horizontal extent of soil region). This limitation is imposed to avoid numerical errors which may result from too many slices, or too small a slip region.

   APPENDIX C


Deformation Analysis
Simplified Procedure for Estimating Earthquake Induced Deviatoric Slope Displacements
by Jonathan D. Bray and Thaleia Travasarou
Journal of Geotechnical and Geonvironmental Engineering, ASCE, V. 133(4), pp. 381-392, April 2007

SEE NOTES BELOW FOR GUIDANCE IN THE USE OF SPREADSHEET

Input Parameters
Yield Coefficient (ky)                                     0.065                 Based on pseudostatic analysis
Initial Fundamental Period (Ts)                             0.06 seconds         1D: Ts=4H/Vs 2D: Ts=2.6H/Vs
Degraded Period (1.5Ts)                                     0.09 seconds
Moment Magnitude (Mw)                                        7.0
Spectral Acceleration ( Sa(1.5Ts) )                         0.78 g

Additional Input Parameters
Probability of Exceedance #1 (P1)                              84 %
Probability of Exceedance #2 (P2)                              50 %
Probability of Exceedance #3 (P3)                              16 %
Displacement Threshold (d_threshold)                            5 cm

Intermediate Calculated Parameters
Non-Zero Seismic Displacement Est (D)                      47.06 cm              eq. (5) or (6)
Standard Deviation of Non-Zero Seismic D                    0.66

Results
Probability of Negligible Displ. (P(D=0))               0.000                    eq. (3)
D1                                                      24.41      cm            calc. using eq. (7)
D2                                                      47.06      cm            calc. using eq. (7)
D3                                                      90.73      cm            calc. using eq. (7)
P(D>d_threshold)                                        1.000                    eq. (7)

Notes
1. Values highlighted in blue are input parameters
2. Probability of Exceedance is the desired probability of exceeding a particular displacement value.
3. Displacements D1, D2, and D3 correspond to P1, P2, and P3, respectively.
   (e.g., the probability of exceeding displacement D1 is P1)
4. Calculated seismic displacements are due to deviatoric deformation only (add in volumetrically induced movement).
5. ky may range between 0.01 and 0.5, Ts between 0 and 2 s, Sa between 0.002 and 2.7 g, M between 4.5 and 9
6. Rigid slope is assumed for Ts < 0.05 s
7. When a value for D is not calculated, D is < 1cm
8. ky may be estimated using the simplified equations shown below.
9. Examples of how Ts is estimated are shown below.
10. Vs = weighted avg. shear wave velocity for the sliding mass, e.g., for 2 layers, Vs = [(h1)(Vs1) + (h2)(Vs2)]/(h1 + h2)

       Re :      Response to Review No. 2
                 Geologic Hazard Evaluation - Geotechnical Slope Stability Analysis
                 Proposed Residential Structure
                 Lot 27, Oak Vista No. 1 at Suncrest
                 14812 South Oak Bluff Place
                 Draper, Utah

       This letter presents Gordon Geotechnical Engineering, Inc. (G 2) response to the Draper City
       reviews of our geological hazards and geotechnical slope stability study for the above
       referenced property dated June 9, 2020, and revised August 26, 2020. The August 26, 2020
       report was accompanied with our response letter to a technical review provided by Simon
       Associates (SA) dated July 21 , 2020 on the behalf of Draper City (Draper City Case No:
       SUBD-000980-2020).

       This present response letter pertains to a subsequent October 2, 2020 SA review comments to
       our August 26, 2020 revised report and our accompanied response to review submitted on the
       same date . In coordination with this review and response, an October 15, 2020 meeting was
       held at the Draper City Offices to discuss and clarify issues among the professional
       stakeholders to adequately complete the studies for this evaluation .        Present at the
       October 15, 2020 meeting were the following individuals:

                 1.        Keith Collier, Draper City Chief Building Official.
                 2.        Brien Maxfield , Draper City Engineering Department.
                 3.        Jennifer Jastremsky, Draper City Planning Department.
                 4.        Justin Fessler, Breen Homes.
                 5.        Patrick Emery, G2 , Geotechnical Consultant.
                 6.        Gordon Culp, G2 , Geotechnical Consultant.
                 7.        Greg Schlenker, G2 , Geologic Consultant.
                 8.        Alan Taylor (TG), Draper City Geotechnical Consultant.
                 9.        David Simon (SA), Draper City Geologic Consultant.

  The following includes the comments from the October 2, 2020 SA review, and our response
  and actions to the SA review comments:

  SA Comment 1

  SA Recommendations Based on the requirements of the Draper City Geologic Hazard
  Ordinance and the technical documentation provided by G2 , SA recommends Draper City not
  consider the G2 submittals complete from a geologic perspective until the following items are
  adequately addressed .

  1. Figure 4, Lidar Analvsis

     SA recommends Draper City request G2 explain the roughly north-south trending solid black
     line with hachures within site boundaries.

  G 2 Response/Action Comment 1

  Figure 4 amended to include an annotated explanation of the roughly north-south trending solid
  black line that represents the location of the block failure head scarp.

  2. Figure 5 - Site Geologic Map

     a.   Figure 5 is not consistent with the trench log (Figure 6) and the geologic cross-section
          (Figure 7). SA recommends Draper City request G2 address the apparent discrepancies
          between the site geologic map (Figure 5), the trench log (Figure 6), and the geologic
          cross-section (Figure 7).

  G2 Response/Action Comment 2a

  Descriptive annotations from the amended unit descriptions from the trench log in Figure 6 are
  made consistent on Figure 5 and Figure 7.

     b.    Figure 5 depicts a solid black line which , according to the Explanation in Figure 5,
          represents "Geologic Contact" between geologic units Tv-a and Tv-b . Based on the
          information provided by G2 to date, SA understands the contact between geologic units
          Tv-a and Tv-b was only observed in the trench . Consequently, the certainty of the
          location of the contact is confined to the trench .

          The conventional geologic practice is to use a solid line for a "geologic contact" where
          the identity or existence of the contact between the geologic units is certain and the
          location accurate; a dashed line for a geologic contact where the identity or existence of
          the contact between the geologic units is either approximate or inferred, and ; to use a



                                                                                              Page 2
__s.re•e•n•H•o•m•e•s----------------ic ■                                     6   2
                                                                                     GORDON
                                                                                     GEOTECHNICAL
  Job No. 224-023-19                                                     ■           ENGINEERING, INC.
  Response to Review No. 2
  November 16, 2020



         queried, dashed line for a geologic contact where the identity or existence of the contact
         between the geologic units is questionable and the location inferred (FGDC, 2006).

         SA recommends Draper City request:

            i.   G2 clarify how the location of the geologic contact was determined across the
                 property.

            ii. G2 modify the geologic contact to reflect the degree of certainty of the location of
                the geologic contact.

             iii . Clarify whether the data from the test pit excavations and the logs of the test pits
                   were utilized in creating Figure 5.

  G 2 Response/Action Comment 2b

  Contact lines amended on Figure 5 to show westward in the revision of the contacUhead scarp
  boundary in the vicinity of Test Pit 1A from our updated geotechnical study submitted
  August 26, 2020.

     c. Figure 5 depicts two black dashed lines near the east end of the trench which, according
        to the Explanation in Figure 5, represent "Tva Unit Boundaries ( covered). "

         i. The conventional geologic practice is to indicate the material/structure concealing the
             geologic contact (Compton, 1985; FGDC , 2006). SA recommends Draper City
             request G2 indicate the concealing unit(s) (i.e., covered by younger geologic units,
             etc.).

         ii. SA is assuming a "Unit Boundary" is synonymous with a geologic contact. According
             to Figure 6, Log of Trench, G2 obtained a strike1 and dip2 of 326 °, 14°W at about
             station 0+45 feet and a strike and dip of 355°, 18°W at about station 0+58 feet .

             "Tva Unit Boundaries (covered)" shown in Figure 5 are shown according to the strike ,
             not incorporate the dip and topography and therefore do not properly depict the
             orientation of the Tv-a/Tv-b on Figure 5. SA recommends Draper City request G2
             address the apparent discrepancy.

  G 2 Response/Action Comment 2c

  Because the certainty of the projection of the unit contact lines (formerly Tva Unit
  Boundaries 2A and 2B, presently amended Qms(Tv) Unit Boundaries 2A and 2B) away from
  the trench, we shortened these lines on Figure 5 to only show these covered unit boundaries
  where they we observed in the trench.

   d. SA recommends Draper City request G2 indicate in Figure 5 the source of geologic data
       for off-site areas along Cross -Section A-A ', such that Figure 5 is consistent with
       Figure 3.

G 2 Response/Action Comment 2d

Reference to Beik's, 2005 geologic mapping of the Lehi Quadrangle (presented on Figure 3 and
referenced on Figure 7) added to geologic description on Figure 5.

   e. The logs of Test Pit TP-1A and TP-2A do not appear consistent with the "Geological
      Descriptions" on Figure 5. SA recommends Draper City request G2 address the
      apparent discrepancy.

G 2 Response/Action Comment 2e

The Test Pits TP-1A and TP-2A were performed as part of a geotechnical study without
geological supervision, however the vertical elevation of the encountered bedrock was used to
support our mapping of the site, including our contact line adjustment discussed for response for
Comment 2b.

3. Figure 6, Log of Trench

   a. Figure 6 is not consistent with the site geologic map (Figure 5) and the geologic cross-
      section (Figure 7). SA recommends Draper City request G2 address the apparent
      discrepancies between the site geologic map (Figure 5), the trench log (Figure 6), and
      the geologic cross-section (Figure 7).

G 2 Response/Action Comment 3a

Figure 6 amended with mapping annotations added to logged trench Unit 1 and Units 2;
whereby Unit 1 annotated Tv - Unit 1 to indicate the volcanic block and ash classification of
Unit 1, and Unit 2 annotated Qms(Tv) - Unit 2 to indicate the landslide classification of Unit 2
deposits.

   b. In Comment 2d of the July 21, 2020, SA letter:

       i. SA noted at about Station 0+60, the contact between Units 3 and 2A/2B is undefined,
           and ;

       ii. SA recommended Draper City request G2 address the apparent discrepancy.

       Based on the Explanation in Figure 6, Log of Trench , Units 2A and 2B are "Volcanic
       weathered ash beds, " (presumably Oligocene-Eocene age from Biek, 2005). Based on


                                                                                           Page 4
__s.re•e•n•H•o•m-es-----------------rc:, ■
  Job No. 224 - 023 - 19                                                 ■
                                                                             6   2
                                                                                     GORDON

  Response to Review No. 2
  November 16, 2020



         conventional logging of pedogenic horizons (Birkeland, and others, 1991; Birkeland ,
         1999; Mccalpin , 2009), SA has assumed:

         i.    The "squiggly lines" extending vertically down from the top of Units 2A and 2B
               represent pedogenic soil horizons forming on Units 2A and 2B, and ;

         ii. The "squiggly lines" extending vertically down from the top of Unit 3, colluvial deposits
              (presumably Holocene to Pleistocene-age), represent a pedogenic soi l horizon
              forming on Unit 3.

         If SA's interpretation of the G2 Log of Trench is reasonable, G2's response to SA
         Comment 2d , which follows , does not appear reasonable :

               "Not a discrepancy, the contact between the Units 3 and 2A/ 2B was observed to be
               truncated by the overlying 'Soil A-B Horizon with Vertical Structures ' in Figure 6. "

         Soils form on a parent material (i .e., rock, sediments, colluvium, etc.) (Birkeland, and
         others , 1991 ; Birkeland , 1999). From a geologic perspective, SA would expect the
         contact between 2A and 2B to be terminated by a geologic unit, perhaps with a
         pedogenic soil. SA recommends Draper City requests G2:

         i. Explain how a pedogenic soil horizon can terminate a geologic contact, and;

         ii.   Clarify which "Soil A-B Horizon with Vertical Structures" is terminating the contact
               between Units 3 and 2A/2B.

  G2 Response/Action Comment 3b

  Figure 6 amended to illustrate the superposition of Unit 3 over Unit 2A and Unit 2B.

     c. According to Figure 7, Geologic Cross-Section, the subsurface east of the west end of
        the trench is above/within the "Projected Failure Plane and Direction of Block
        Movement." Consequently, SA would anticipate the area above/within the "Projected
        Fa ilu re Plane and Direction of Block Movement," to be "landslide material ," landslide
        deposits , "landslide block," etc.

         Geologic units 2A and 2B, west of the "Deformed Zone" in Figure 6, Log of Trench , are
         shown as "Volcanic weathered ash beds, " not as "landslide material ," landslide deposits ,
         or "landslide block, " etc. SA recommends Draper City request G2 clarify the apparent
         discrepancy.

  G 2 Response/Action Comment 3b

  Figure 6 amended to label the "Deformed Zone" logged between Unit 1 and Unit 2A on Figure 6
  as "Deformed Zone (Failure Plane)", to illustrate this location on the trench log.

  4. Figure 7, Geologic Cross-Section A-A '

     a. Figure 7 is not consistent with the site geologic map (Figure 5) and the trench log
        (Figure 6). SA recommends Draper City request G2 address the apparent discrepancies
        between the site geologic map (Figure 5), the trench log (Figure 6), and the geologic
        cross-section (Figure 7).

  G 2 Response/Action Comment 4a

  Figure 7 amended to show unit annotations to be consistent with the site geologic map
  (Figure 5) and the trench log (Figure 6).

     b. G2 Figure 7, depicts a "Projected Failure Plane and Direction of Block Movement." The
        "Projected Failure Plane and Direction of Block Movement" northeast of the exposures in
        the G2 trench (Figure 6) was apparently estimated by G2 by evaluation of geologic
        conditions and G2 's slope stability analyses.

         i. The conventional geologic practice is to use a solid line for a "geologic contact" where
             the identity or existence of the contact between the geologic units is certain and the
             location accurate; a dashed line for a geologic contact where the identity or existence
             of the contact between the geologic units is either approximate or inferred, and; to
             use a queried, dashed line for a geologic contact where the identity or existence of
             the contact between the geologic units is questionable and the location inferred
             (FGDC, 2006).

             Consequently, the line depicting the "Projected Failure Plane and Direction of Block
             Movement" below the bottom of the trench on the geologic cross section should
             perhaps be dashed and queried . SA recommends Draper City request G2 modify
             the geologic contact to reflect the certainty of the contact location .

         ii. The area above the Projected Fai lure Plane is labeled as "Ash Deposits (TV) Volcanic
              Rocks of the east Traverse Mountains, undivided. " SA presumes the area above the
              "Projected Failure Plane ," would be "landslide material ," landslide deposits,
              "landslide block," etc. , and designated accordingly. SA recommends Draper City
              request G2 clarify the geologic units above the Projected Failure Plane in Figure.

         a. The 1958 and 1962 photographs (electronic images being sufficient).

         b. A 1958 and a 1962 photograph depicting the subject property (i.e ., one of each) .

     Consequently, SA recommends Draper City requests G2 provide:

         a. The 1958 and 1962 photographs (electronic images would be adequate).

     b. A 1958 and a1962 photograph depicting the subject property (i .e. , one of each) .

  delivered to Mr. Simon during the October 15, 2020 meeting .
